471. THOMAS MAISEY was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of December, 1 bed, value 20s.; 1 carpet, value 3l. 10s.; 4 sheets, value 18s.; 2 curtains, value 2s.; 2 tablecloths, value 8s.; 6 napkins, value 5s.; 4 coats, value 12s., and 3 blankets, value 13s., the goods of Emanuel Moses.
EMANUEL MOSES. The prisoner was employed by me at times, and on the 27th of December I sent him to Edwards' auction-room, Vere-street, Clare-market, to fetch these goods, which I had bought on the 26th; I waited at the auction-room for him, but he did not come; I wrapped them in a carpet, and left word for them to deliver them to him if he came - I saw him afterwards, and he said he had not been able to go; I told him to go next day.
WILLIAM HENRY SLADE. I am a coachman. On the 27th of December the prisoner hired me at the corner of Dean-street, Tooley-street; he put these things into the coach at the King's Head public-house, to go to Petticoat-lane - when we got there he said he did not know where to go; I thought he was to meet a person there to buy the things - he then told me to drive to Tooley-street again, and when I was in Whitechapel it was between eleven and twelve o'clock at night; I did not know the turning, and asked the watchman the way - the prisoenr told me to go on to Cornhill, and there a man spoke to the prisoner, and got in - they ordered me back to Aldgate again - they got out there, and told me to wait; and in a few minutes a man came, and told me the goods were stolen; the prisoner came up - I asked him about them - he said they were his master's; I told an officer, who took us all to the watch-house.
MICHAEL MEREDITH. I attend the sale-room in Vere-street; Moses bought these articles, and told me to deliver them to a person who would mention his name; I delivered all of them to the prisoner on the 27th; he left another bed, as he could not carry it.
WILLIAM IVEY. I am an apprentice to Mr. Reeve, pawnbroker, of Snowhill. I have two blankets, pawned on the 27th of December, by a woman.
JOHN ROBERTS. I am an officer. I received information, and detained the coach and goods; I saw the prisoner there; he said they were his sister's, who lived in King's Head-yard, Tooley-street; I asked what he was going to do with them - he did not seem to know; this duplicate of the blankets was found on him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I thought I would leave the things at my sister's, and then took them to Petticoat-lane.
GUILTY. Aged 60.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18280110-217]
472. JOHN STANTON and JAMES HAYDON were indicted for stealing, on the 5th of January, 1 pair of boots, value 4s., the goods of George Joseph Ford.
GEORGE JOSEPH FORD. I live in Field-lane. On the 5th of January, in the evening, I saw the prisoners pass my window two or three times, and told my shopman and apprentice to look after them; these boots were taken from the board at the window - I went out - found Stanton with them under his arm, and Haydon by his side; they were then going up a court into Holborn; I had seen the boots safe ten minutes before; Stanton said, "Let me go, it will be all right;" I took him, and my shopman took Haydon.
THOMAS LONG. I am a watchman. I saw the prisoners go up Field-lane twice, between eight and nine o'clock in the evening, and the second time I heard an alarm that they had stolen the boots; I assisted in taking them to the watch-house; when I got up, Stanton had dropped one boot, and Mr. Ford was taking it up.
JOHN EDWARDS. I received the prisoners in charge at the watch-house; they said they were going to the play, but we found only a few halfpence on them.
STANTON'S Defence. I had been cleaning a chimney which was on fire, and got intoxicated afterwards, and did not know what I was doing.
HAYDON'S Defence. I know nothing about them.
STANTON - GUILTY. Aged 27.
Transported for Seven Years.
HAYDON - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18280110-218]
473. ROBERT TURNER was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of December, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Edward Janson, from his person.
EDWARD JANSON. On the 31st of December I was in Whitechapel, about noon - I crossed over at Houndsditch - some persons called to me; I turned round, and saw the officer with my handkerchief in one hand, and the prisoner in the other; I had not noticed him before - my handkerchief was taken from my coat pocket.
FRANCIS KEYS. I am an officer. I was on duty, and saw the prisoner, and two others make an attempt at the prosecutor's pocket, just opposite Petticoat-lane - they then went on, and closed up to him by Aldgate church, and I saw the prisoner put something into his breast - I crossed and took him with the handkerchief.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you see his hand at the prosecutor's pocket? A. No; but they were all together; I did not know him before.
Prisoner's Defence. I had been after a situation at Mr. Baker's; I picked the handkerchief up.
MR. JANSON. It was a muddy day - there was no dirt on the handkerchief.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280110-219]
474. MARY HALEY was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of December, 2 pairs of boots, value 5s., the goods Robert Langford.
THOMAS DAVIS. I live with Robert Langford, a boot and shoe-maker, of Cripplegate. I did not see these boots taken, but I had seen them two minutes before, on a horse in the shop - the prisoner came in to look at a pair of boy's boots; I showed her a pair - she did not like them; she offered 3s. 6d. for another pair, which I could not take - she left, and came back, and asked for a pair of boy's spencers, which were in a case, and while I got them, she must have put these boots into her apron: Sarah Davis came up, and asked what was in her apron; she said nothing - but these boots were found in it.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Was she not talking to you about boots at this very time? A. No.
SARAH DAVIS. I was coming down stairs, and saw the prisoner take two pairs of boots off the horse, and put them into her apron; I asked what she had there; she said nothing - but I took them from her apron.
JOSEPH ORTON. I am an officer, and took her in charge- I found 4s. on her - she is married, and has two children.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Seven Days.
[trialAccount t18280110-220]
475. DAVID REES was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of December, 1 canvas bag, value 6d.; 4 sovereigns, 2 shillings, and 1 sixpence, the property of John Jones, from his person.
JOHN JONES. On the 14th of December, about half-past six o'clock in the evening, I was at the door of the Lock and Key public-house, in Smithfield; my purse, containing this money, was tied up, and in my breeches pocket - the prisoner came up, and said, "John Jones, how are you this long time?" I said middling; he asked if I would stand treat; I said No; but I had a pot of beer, and he was welcome to that; he asked me to lend him a shilling- I took out the purse - he snatched it, and ran away - he was not stopped.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Do you mean to say you knew the prisoner before? A. Yes; I was quite sober; I ran as far as Long-lane, and then missed him; I did not call Stop thief! the Magistrate did not ask if I made an alarm, nor did I tell him - I thought it was done in fun, and expected he would come back; the prisoner had high shoes on - they were half-boots.
DAVID EVANS. I saw Jones at the Lock and Key on the 15th; he described the prisoner to me, and I took him- he tried to get from me - I took Jones to the prisoner's house; I asked the prisoner if he knew Jones; he said No; I called the prosecutor, who said to him, "Give me the money, that is all I want;" he said he would swear he was the man; I found nothing on him; he said he never saw Jones in his life; but afterwards said he had known him twenty-seven years ago.
Cross-examined. Q. Have you and the prisoner ever quarrelled? A. Yes, once - I had him once before a Magistrate.
ABRAHAM GRIFFITHS. I went with Evans to apprehend the prisoner on the 15th of December - Evans asked if he knew Coran, I think - it was a Welch name, which I forget - it was not Jones.
Cross-examined. Q. Did he make any resistance? A. Yes, when we got to Smithfield he did.
Witnesses for the Defence.
THOMAS GODDARD. I am a green-grocer, and live at No. 23, Woodbridge-street. I have known the prisoner thirteen or fourteen years. On the evening of the 14th of December, at a quarter to five o'clock I went to the prisoner's house, and asked him what time it was; he told me about five; I went in, drew the clock up, came out again to smoke my pipe, and the parish clock struck five; I walked up stairs, and saw the prisoner looking out of his window, with a night-cap on; I called him; he came down; we stood looking at the men killing sheep, and were together till a quarter or half-past seven o'clock.
COURT. Q. Can you be sure you saw him from five to seven o'clock? A. Yes. He was taken up the next day.
FRANCIS ROBINSON. I am a glass-cutter, and live in White-cross-street. I have known the prisoner six or seven years. On Friday the 14th of December I took him a letter from his sister, who lives in my house, at a quarter to four o'clock, and staid with him and Goddard till ten minutes past seven.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280110-221]
476. JOHN JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of December, 1 seal, value 1s.; 1 watch-key, value 5s. 6d.; and part of a watch-chain, value 6d., the goods of Richard Sellers Yarnall, from his person.
RICHARD SELLERS YARNALL. On the evening of the 23d of December I was at the corner of Short-street, Moorfields - a man, in the habit of a sailor, who I believe was the prisoner, came up, made a snatch at my watch, and got part of the chain, seal, and key - I had a heavy bundle, and could not pursue very quick; I lost sight of him, but my son and other persons pursued, and in about three minutes the prisoner was brought back.
WILLIAM SEAL YARNALL. I am the prosecutor's son. I saw the prisoner snatch at my father's watch - he ran away; I pursued him down a street and down a court, but lost sight of him; I saw him stopped by an officer, whom I had seen before I lost sight of the prisoner.
Prisoner. Q. When the Alderman asked how you knew me, what did you say? A. I said I knew you by your clothes, but not by your face.
JOHN ATTERWELL. I am an officer. I was in Little Moorfields. I heard a cry of Stop thief! a moment after I passed Buckley's-alley - I turned and pursued the prisoner from the alley to Crow-court, and there took him; a man came up, and said, "That is not the man," but I knew he was, for I had never lost sight of him - I heard the cry, saw him crossing, and go down Buckley's-alley - I secured him in less than three minutes.
JOHN WEATHERALL. I am a patrol. I was with Alterwell; we just passed the end of Short-street, and heard the cry of Stop thief! I saw a man run down Buckley's-alley, I pursued, and saw Atterwell with the prisoner - he was dressed as a sailor, and no one else was there.
Prisoner's Defence. I had been at the Red Lion public-house, and as I returned home saw several persons together; there was an alarm of Stop thief! I pursued, and was taken; several persons came up, and said I was not the man; the officer said, "I think it is him."
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18280110-222]
477. WILLIAM MATTHEWS was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of January, 1 truck, value 63s., the goods of Ralph Rossi.
RALPH ROSSI. I am a wine-merchant. I was packing in my warehouse about seven o'clock on the 2d of January - my boy went to the door, and called Stop thief! I went out, missed the truck, and found the prisoner with it almost directly.
THOMAS EVERITT. I am in Mr. Rossi's employ. I opened the warehouse door, hearing a noise, and saw the prisoner going along with the truck - he left it, and ran; I pursued, and never lost sight of him.
Prisoner. He came up and asked if I was not the man- I waited at a public-house for five minutes; the boy said he beleived it was me; the officer said it would not do to believe - then he said he was sure.
THOMAS EVERITT. The first thing I said to him was,"What are you doing with that truck?" he said he didnot touch it; I left him at the public-house door, and went back to master.
RALPH ROSSI. I did not wish to prosecute him, and left him at the public-house, but some persons gave him in charge.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18280110-223]
478. WILLIAM WATSON was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of January, 7 sovereigns, and 6 half-sovereigns, the monies of William Banks, in his dwelling-house.
WILLIAM BANKS. I live in Blakeley's-rents, Bartholomew-close, and am a publican; the prisoner was formerly my pot-boy, for about four months, and had quitted me three or four months; he had been at my house two or three times since, and was there on the Friday before, the 6th of January. On the Saturday, at half-past twelve o'clock at night, my money was all right in a little galleypot - I locked it up in a cupboard in the dining-room, and had the key in my pocket - there were seven sovereigns and, I think, six half-sovereigns. On Sunday night, at half-past ten o'clock, I was going to put some more to it - I found the cupboard unlocked, the gally-pot removed, and the money gone - I have never found any of it since; I inquired who had been up stairs, and could not find that any body had, except the prisoner, who had gone up at two o'clock, to shift his shirt, but he had not asked my leave, and had no right there. I had him taken about half-past eleven o'clock that night - he said he knew nothing of it, and had no gold or any money about him - I am sure of that; he was searched in my presence, and 1l. 2s. 6d. in silver, and some halfpence were found on him.
REBECCA DARTSSE. I was working at the prosecutor's on the 6th of January - I saw the prisoner there about two o'clock; he asked me for a shirt - I aired it, and gave it him; he went up stairs to put it on, and brought down his dirty one.
GEORGE SMITH. I am tapster to the prosecutor. On Sunday, the 6th of January, I saw the prisoner with his shirt on his hand, going towards the stairs; he came down again in ten minutes or a quarter of an hour - there had been several persons there on the Saturday night, and there were five or six persons in the house on Sunday.
CHARLES BANNISTER. I am a servant out of place. On the 6th of January I was at the Tavistock Arms public-house, Tavistock-place, Brunswick-square, between six and seven o'clock in the evening; the prisoner came in and called for a quartern of gin - I knew him before, and had part of it; he paid for it with a half-sovereign; he had it in a little bag - he put his fingers into the bag, and said he was not without money, and I saw, I thought, ten or twelve sovereigns in it.
HENRY BUTT. I am pot-boy at the Hand and Chair public-house, Colonade. The prisoner came there about seven o'clock, and had some refreshment; I saw him open a little bag with six or seven sovereings in it.
RICHARD DAVIS. I was constable of the night; the prisoner was brought to the watch-house about half-past eleven o'clock on the 6th of January; I found on him 23s. in silver, and 1s. 2d. in copper. He said he lodged in Coram-street.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, denying the charge.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280110-224]
First London Jury - before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
479. WALTER WEBB was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of December, 250lbs, weight of cork, value 5l. 10s., the goods of William Peter McAndrews.
WILLIAM PETER McANDREWS. I am a fruit-merchant, in partnership with my brother, and live in London-street. I employed the prisoner to discharge, unload, and warehouse a quantity of cork, which I had purchased; the cork in question was in a cellar in George-lane; the whole delivery was complete on Saturday, the 8th of December, and we discharged the prisoner that evening; he had superintended the delivery, and had men under him; his accounts were all settled that evening; and on the Monday I gave him in charge, on suspicion of stealing cork from George-lane; he had nothing to do with cork after the 8th.
Cross-examined. Q. When did you speak to him about this? A. On the Tuesday; he came to my house with oranges - I gave him in charge.
ALFRED WALKER. I live with my parents, in George-lane, and work at a jewellers, in Great Queen-street; the prosecutor's cellar is under our house. On Monday, the 10th of December, about a quarter to nine o'clock, the prisoner came and asked for a light; my brother was at the door with me; he brought a truck, went into the cellar, and took away three bundles of cork on the truck; he had been there before, about cork; the cellar was not locked; my father has the care of it; the door is inside the house.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. Does not your father keep the key? A. There is no lock; it is bolted; we have two lodgers - I had never seen him there before myself; he had a fustain coat on, I believe. When I was at the Mansion-house, I was desired to look among the prisoners for the man, and it was five minutes before I fixed on him - I passed him several times; my brother pointed him out, and then I knew him.
COURT. Q. Can you now say, you are sure he is the man? A. Yes - I always said so.
WILLIAM WALKER. I am nine years old, and live with my father in George-lane. On Monday, the 10th of December, a man came with a truck, and took away three bundles of cork; the prisoner is that man, he asked me for a light, and I gave it to him.
Cross-examined. Q. How often had you seen him before? A. On the Friday and Saturday; he had a fustian jacket on; my brother was with me.
DAVID WRIGHT. I am a ship-agent, and live in Thames-street. I had a truck in my care on the 10th of December; it was missing that morning, from about nine till near twelve o'clock - I live about a quarter of a mile from George-lane. I know nothing of the prisoner.
JOHN HALL. I am a fruit-cooper. On Monday morning, the 10th of December, I saw the prisoner with a truck, and three bundles of cork in it, going up Pudding-lane, close to George-lane, it was about nine o'clock in the morning.
Cross-examined. Q. How long have you known him? A. Two years.
JOSEPH ROME. I live in George-lane. On Monday, the 10th of December, I heard a noise, and saw a truck opposite No. 3 - the prisoner brought out three bundles of cork singly - I believe him to be the man, but will not swear to him.
JOHN BAYLEY. I am an officer, and took him in charge.
Prisoner's Defence. I know myself innocent.
Three witnesses gave the prisoner a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18280110-225]
480. PETER CRAWLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of December, 2 boots, value 16s., the goods of Anthony Smith Johnson.
JOSEPH SOUTHEY. I am shopman to Anthony Smith Johnson, of Aldersgate-street. About six o'clock in the evening of the 26th of December, I was under the windowboard, and could not be seen; the prisoner came into the shop, looked round for about a minute, took a pair of boots, and walked away; I cried Stop thief! twice, and pursued him; he dropped them at the corner of Long-lane, crossed over, and turned round to walk towards Aldersgate-street again, and was taken - I never lost sight of him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOHN BUCE. I am a patrol. I heard the alarm, and took the prisoner.
ANTHONY SMITH JOHNSON. I keep this shop, and was in the parlour at the time.
Prisoner's Defence. I had just left my lodgings, and at the top of Long-lane I crossed the road; Southey came up, and said I had stolen the boots; a gentleman and lady gave me their address, and said they would prove I was not the man - they were here last night, and are gone into the country.
ANTHONY SMITH JOHNSON. A lady and gentleman came into the shop, and left their card with the prisoner.
JOSEPH SOUTHEY. I did not hear them say the prisoner was not the man.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18280110-226]
Fifth Middlesex Jury - Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
481. ELIZA PHILLIPS was indicted for a burglary in the dwelling-house of Maria Fitzgibbon, about eleven o'clock on the night of the 13th of December, with intent to steal, and stealing therein 2 sheets, value 5s., the goods of Thomas Parker; and 6 gowns, value 20s.; 2 shawls, value 10s.; 4 petticoats, value 12s.; 2 handkerchiefs, value 2s.; 1 shift, value 2s.; 1 night-gown, value 1s.; 1 veil, value 2s., and 5 collars, value 1s., the goods of the said Maria Fitzgibbon.
MARIA FITGIBBON. I am single, and lodged in a back garret, in New-street, St. Giles'. I am a servant, out of place; the landlord did not live in the house - his name is Parker. I went out at eleven o'clock at night of the 13th of December, on business, leaving my key with Mrs. Parker; I returned at twelve o'clock - the street door is kept on the latch - I found the hasp of my lock had been drawn out, but the landlord had put it on again; I went in, and missed the articles stated in the indictment. I knew the prisoner - she had visited me once in that room; I sometimes do needle-work, and when I cannot get it, I sometimes get my living in the street, I am sorry to say.
THOMAS PARKER. I let this house out in separate lodgings, and live next door; Fitzgibbon left her key with my wife, about eleven o'clock, and in about an hour Driscoll came in, in his shirt, and gave an alarm; I took a candle, went up stairs at this house, to the back garret, and saw the prisoner on the landing-place, and all these articles strewed about - she had no business there - she had no property in her possession; she called to me directly I got up, and begged me to forgive her, and let her go; I said she had broken into the room and stolen the things, and I could not let her go - I found an iron pin, about six steps from the room-door - the staple appeared to have been drawn out by that pin; no other women lodge there.
DENNIS DRISCOLL. I am a labourer, and lodge at this house - I was at home at eleven o'clock at night - I went to bed about eleven, and in about half an hour heard a noise, like the breaking open of the staple of a door - I listened, then went up in the dark, and found the prisoner there; all the things laid about - I called for a light, and went for the landlord; the things were not packed up - I held her till the light came - I know nothing about the iron pin.
JAMES DOODY. I am an officer. I was sent for to the house. I went up, and took the prisoner into custody - I found the property on the landing, and some part inside the door.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I have known her two years - she is a prostitute, like myself - we once lived together; I only went up to see if she was at home - the man came up, and said I came to rob the house.
MARIA FILZGIBBON. We never lived together. I have walked with her, and we have drank together; I was in service five weeks ago, for about half a year.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280110-227]
482. HENRY HEATH and HENRY PARSONS were indicted for stealing, on the 10th of January, 18 penny-pieces, and 21 halfpence, the monies of William Ashby.
HEATH pleaded GUILTY.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
PARSONS pleaded GUILTY.
Confined Two Months, and Whipped.
